I mean, my clothes are definitely feeling tighter, especially around the waist, but I haven't actually stepped on a scale in a while. So, I couldn't give you an exact number or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I know I'm probably putting on some pounds, but it's not something I sit around stressing over. It's more just the... the feeling out of control that bothers me, not the weight itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just going to be one bowl of pozole. But before I know it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. After I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and wrappers, and I feel... I just feel completely disgusted with myself. \n\nI've always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man. But when I'm sitting there looking at the mess I just made, I feel like I've lost all that control. It's just not who I want to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually do anything to try to undo or make up for what you've eaten after these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I mean, I don't... I don't make myself throw up or take pills or anything, if that's what you mean. And I'm driving a route most of the day, so I'm definitely not out there exercising it off. \n\nI mostly just... I just sit there with it. I just deal with feeling sick and guilty, and that's about it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating out of control happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since I’ve been home with a back injury, it’s been happening a lot more. I'd say it happens several nights most weeks. \n\nIf I really think about it, looking back over these last three months, I... I really can’t think of a single week where I didn’t have at least one of those episodes. It's just been pretty constant lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been tracking it, to be honest. I mean, my clothes are definitely feeling tighter, especially around the waist, but I haven't actually stepped on a scale in a while. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because my eating has gotten really out of hand. What really brings me in today is how I feel afterward. After I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and wrappers, and I feel... I just feel completely disgusted with myself. \n\nI've always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man. But when I'm sitting there looking at the mess I just made, I feel like I've lost all that control. It's just not who I want to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually do anything to try to undo or make up for what you've eaten after these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I mean, I don't... I don't make myself throw up or take pills or anything, if that's what you mean. And I'm driving a route most of the day, so I'm definitely not out there exercising it off. \n\nI mostly just... I just sit there with it. I just deal with feeling sick and guilty, and that's about it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating out of control happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since I’ve been home with a back injury, it’s been happening a lot more. I'd say it happens several nights most weeks. \n\nIf I really think about it, looking back over these last three months, I... I really can’t think of a single week where I didn’t have at least one of those episodes. It's just been pretty constant lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been tracking it, to be honest. I mean, my clothes are definitely feeling tighter, especially around the waist, but I haven't actually stepped on a scale in a while. So, I couldn't give you an exact number or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I know I'm probably putting on some pounds, but it's not something I sit around stressing over. It's more just the... the feeling out of control that bothers me, not the weight itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just going to be one bowl of pozole. But before I know it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating out of control is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I spend a lot of time on the road, and sure, that can get pretty monotonous, but... I don't really eat like that just because I'm bored. It doesn't seem connected to that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating out of control is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I spend a lot of time on the road, and sure, that can get pretty monotonous, but... I don't really eat like that just because I'm bored. It doesn't seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel upset or emotional before it happens. I mean, I'm just going about my day, usually. I haven't really noticed any strong feelings or anything leading up to it. It just kind of starts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Never bundle several symptoms, or several attributes of one symptom, into a single question — e.g. asking whether a symptom is present and how long it has lasted at the same time.\n- If you need to ask about more than one thing, ask the one that tells you the most now and come back to the rest later.\n- Keep the question clear enough for the patient to understand.\n- Respond in English.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"intent\": \"<what you want to find out with this question>\", \"question\": \"<how you actually ask it in the interview>\"}\n"

From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n16\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF50.8\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Well, I guess I'm here because my eating has gotten really out of hand. What really brings me in today is how I feel afterward. After I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and wrappers, and I feel... I just feel completely disgusted with myself. \n\nI've always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man. But when I'm sitting there looking at the mess I just made, I feel like I've lost all that control. It's just not who I want to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually do anything to try to undo or make up for what you've eaten after these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I mean, I don't... I don't make myself throw up or take pills or anything, if that's what you mean. And I'm driving a route most of the day, so I'm definitely not out there exercising it off. \n\nI mostly just... I just sit there with it. I just deal with feeling sick and guilty, and that's about it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating out of control happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since I’ve been home with a back injury, it’s been happening a lot more. I'd say it happens several nights most weeks. \n\nIf I really think about it, looking back over these last three months, I... I really can’t think of a single week where I didn’t have at least one of those episodes. It's just been pretty constant lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been tracking it, to be honest. I mean, my clothes are definitely feeling tighter, especially around the waist, but I haven't actually stepped on a scale in a while. So, I couldn't give you an exact number or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I know I'm probably putting on some pounds, but it's not something I sit around stressing over. It's more just the... the feeling out of control that bothers me, not the weight itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just going to be one bowl of pozole. But before I know it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating out of control is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I spend a lot of time on the road, and sure, that can get pretty monotonous, but... I don't really eat like that just because I'm bored. It doesn't seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel upset or emotional before it happens. I mean, I'm just going about my day, usually. I haven't really noticed any strong feelings or anything leading up to it. It just kind of starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any emotional arousal, such as excitement or tension, right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't get excited about it, and I wouldn't say I feel tense, either. Like I mentioned a second ago, I don't really notice any kind of build-up or strong feelings right before it happens. It's not really like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating out of control where you didn't feel guilty or disgusted with yourself afterward?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever had a time where I didn't feel that way. Honestly, that's exactly what brings me in to see you. After I do it, I just... I sit there looking at all the empty wrappers and containers, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. After I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and wrappers, and I feel... I just feel completely disgusted with myself. \n\nI've always tried to be a steady, in-control kind of man. But when I'm sitting there looking at the mess I just made, I feel like I've lost all that control. It's just not who I want to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually do anything to try to undo or make up for what you've eaten after these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, I don't do anything like that. I mean, I don't... I don't make myself throw up or take pills or anything, if that's what you mean. And I'm driving a route most of the day, so I'm definitely not out there exercising it off. \n\nI mostly just... I just sit there with it. I just deal with feeling sick and guilty, and that's about it.\n\nDoctor: How often do these episodes of eating out of control happen?\n\nPatient: Well, since I’ve been home with a back injury, it’s been happening a lot more. I'd say it happens several nights most weeks. \n\nIf I really think about it, looking back over these last three months, I... I really can’t think of a single week where I didn’t have at least one of those episodes. It's just been pretty constant lately.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight over the past few months?\n\nPatient: I haven't really been tracking it, to be honest. I mean, my clothes are definitely feeling tighter, especially around the waist, but I haven't actually stepped on a scale in a while. So, I couldn't give you an exact number or anything.\n\nDoctor: Do you worry about gaining weight?\n\nPatient: Not really. I mean, I know I'm probably putting on some pounds, but it's not something I sit around stressing over. It's more just the... the feeling out of control that bothers me, not the weight itself.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever eat an amount of food that is larger than what most people would eat in a similar time period?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I definitely do. Some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just going to be one bowl of pozole. But before I know it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating out of control is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I spend a lot of time on the road, and sure, that can get pretty monotonous, but... I don't really eat like that just because I'm bored. It doesn't seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel upset or emotional before it happens. Some evenings I’ll heat up what I swear is just going to be one bowl of pozole. But before I know it, I’ve gone through the whole pot. And then I'll have a couple tamales, some chips, and... really just whatever leftovers are sitting there in the fridge. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. It ends up looking like I’m feeding a whole crew instead of just myself.\n\nDoctor: Is your feeling of disgust after eating mostly because you feel like you've lost control, or is it also because you're worried about what it might do to your weight?\n\nPatient: It's definitely the control. Like I said, the weight isn't really what's on my mind. It's just... once I start, it feels like the same discipline I have when I'm out on the road just completely shuts off. I'll be sitting there, actually telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' but my hand just keeps going back for more anyway. That's the part that makes me feel so disgusted with myself.\n\nDoctor: Is the disgust you feel after eating more about the act of eating so much or about other things, like how it reflects on your character?\n\nPatient: It's really about what it says about me, I guess. That's actually what brings me in here. After I do it, I just sit there looking at the empty containers and the mess, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It makes me feel like... like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always prided myself on being. It feels like a failure of character, honestly.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel guilty while you are eating during these episodes, or is the guilt something you feel afterward?\n\nPatient: It's definitely afterward. While I'm eating, I'm just... I'm not really thinking about it. But like I was saying, what brings me in is that after I do it, I just sit there looking at all the empty containers and the mess, and that's when the guilt hits. I just feel so disgusted with myself, like I'm not the steady, in-control man I've always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually eat this way when you're feeling stressed, anxious, or upset?\n\nPatient: I don't really think so. I mean, I haven't noticed a pattern like that. Sure, the road can be stressful sometimes, but... this happens even when I'm just having a normal, quiet night at home. So I haven't really tied it to feeling upset or anxious.\n\nDoctor: Do you usually feel hungry before these episodes of eating out of control?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I'm hungry. I mean, sometimes I've just had a full meal not that long before it happens. It's not about my stomach growling or needing food... it's just this urge to eat that comes out of nowhere, regardless of whether I'm actually hungry or not.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're eating really quickly during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, definitely. It's like... well, it's like I'm on a tight delivery schedule or something. I eat so fast that I barely even taste what I'm having. I mean, sometimes I'll catch myself actually breathing hard because I'm just shoveling the food in without slowing down at all.\n\nDoctor: Do you think your eating out of control is ever triggered by feeling bored?\n\nPatient: I don't think so, no. I mean, I spend a lot of time on the road, and sure, that can get pretty monotonous, but... I don't really eat like that just because I'm bored. It doesn't seem connected to that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel upset or emotional right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel upset or emotional before it happens. I mean, I'm just going about my day, usually. I haven't really noticed any strong feelings or anything leading up to it. It just kind of starts.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel any emotional arousal, such as excitement or tension, right before these episodes of eating happen?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I don't get excited about it, and I wouldn't say I feel tense, either. Like I mentioned a second ago, I don't really notice any kind of build-up or strong feelings right before it happens. It's not really like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had an episode of eating out of control where you didn't feel guilty or disgusted with yourself afterward?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think I've ever had a time where I didn't feel that way. Honestly, that's exactly what brings me in to see you. After I do it, I just... I sit there looking at all the empty wrappers and containers, and I feel completely disgusted with myself. It really makes me feel like I’m not the steady, in-control man I’ve always tried to be.\n\nDoctor: Have these episodes of eating out of control been happening for more than 3 months?\n\nPatient: Right about three months, yeah. This has all been going on since I strained my back and got taken off the road for work... so that was just about three months ago.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel a sense of pleasure or euphoria while you're eating during these episodes?\n\nPatient: No, definitely not. I don't feel any pleasure from it at all. It's not enjoyable. I just eat and eat, and I don't feel good while it's happening... or after.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're unable to stop eating or control how much you're eating once you've started during these episodes?\n\nPatient: Yeah, that's exactly it. Once I start, it just feels like the same discipline I had out on the road completely shuts off. Even while it's happening and I'm sitting there telling myself, 'Magin, put the fork down,' my hand just keeps going back for more. I really can't seem to stop it once it starts.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"
